{"parentUuid":"bfa40dd0-e7f9-43c9-8940-6f04051577c0","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"58479281-f0fc-437a-b3c2-51154bb8cfd1","agentId":"a3d0211873f170d2c","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01H7MebuY8dyb3cEwLaupwpf","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# SIGIL spine bounded-growth — DESIGN BRIEF (distilled from the understand phase)\n2\t\n3\tThe SIGIL spine is an append-only, hash-chained, Ed25519-signed JSONL log at `~/.sigil/spine/spine.jsonl`\n4\t(93 MB / 43,412 records, growing). GOAL: bound its growth / per-file size / read latency **without\n5\tweakening tamper-evidence or the append-only guarantee**. 34 consumers across 464 call sites read it;\n6\t23 do FULL scans; 9 need old-seq resolution. Full raw analysis (invariants, per-consumer contract,\n7\ttamper-conflict, durability, critique) is in `/tmp/claude-1000/-home-kali-Pictures-PENTEST-main/7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27/tasks/w705miq65.output` — read it for detail. Code is at `/home/kali/sigil/sigil/spine/*` and `sigil/reuse/{chain,canonical,models}.py`.\n8\t\n9\t## The 19 invariants a design MUST preserve (condensed)\n10\t\n11\t1. **chain-anchored-from-genesis** — `verify_chain` seeds `prev=_GENESIS_PREV` and rejects entries[0] if\n12\t   its prev_hash != genesis. (It does NOT require entries[0].seq==0 — only prev==genesis + contiguity —\n13\t   so a rebased chain at seq K is valid IFF K's prev_hash is rewritten to a genesis-equivalent value.)\n14\t2. **strict-seq-contiguity** — seq[i]==seq[i-1]+1, no gaps/dupes. Prune may only remove a CONTIGUOUS\n15\t   PREFIX, never punch interior holes.\n16\t3. **seq-is-immutable-primary-key** — never renumber; entry_hash binds seq; parent_id/supersedes_id/\n17\t   cursors/graph anchors reference seq. Keep original seqs across any compaction.\n18\t4. **signed-head-entry_count-is-the-anti-truncation-gate** — `classify_head` flags `len(entries) <\n19\t   head.entry_count` as TAMPERING; entry_count is ABSOLUTE. THE central conflict: prune ⇒ shorter ⇒ reads\n20\t   as rollback. Any prune MUST re-sign a snapshot-aware head atomically.\n21\t5. **monotonic-last_seq anti-rollback** — tip seq never decreases (verify_head prev_highwater [dormant on\n22\t   checkpoint path]; SpineTailer._high_water in-memory). Front-prune keeps tip seq ⇒ safe; renumber ⇒\n23\t   false trip + real-truncation-indistinguishable.\n24\t6. **append-only / offsets-never-move / no-in-place-rewrite** — never rewrite in place; build-then-\n25\t   os.replace. In-place same-or-larger rewrite is UNDETECTED by the size-only index heuristic ⇒ silent\n26\t   wrong reads.\n27\t7. **torn-tail-truncate-before-append (BLOCK-1)** — must run on the ACTIVE append segment only; a sealed\n28\t   segment that ends torn will never be truncated by a future append ⇒ permanent false verify() gap\n29\t   (critique A2: rotation must run `_last_valid_boundary` truncate at SEAL time).\n30\t8. **fsync durability** — acked append fsync'd; head swapped via temp+fsync+os.replace+dir-fsync\n31\t   (`checkpoint._atomic_write_text`). Every rotation output must match this.\n32\t9. **index extend/rebuild correctness under external mutation** — detection is SIZE-ONLY today; must\n33\t   become IDENTITY/EPOCH-based (st_dev/st_ino + manifest generation), and segment-aware (key by\n34\t   (segment,offset)). Rotation must not read as an in-place shrink (which is a truncation attack).\n35\t10. **replay-stable content digest (ts excluded)** — any snapshot/manifest record must digest via the\n36\t    same `append()` content shape + canonical_json (ts excluded) or its binding check fails.\n37\t11. **two-layer unkeyed integrity vs the keyed signed head** — a compactor is a recompute-capable writer;\n38\t    verify() alone can't certify a compaction. The owner-signed head is the authority; compaction must be\n39\t    owner-key-gated.\n40\t12. **read-path presents ONE contiguous genesis chain** — entries()/iter_records(-1)/verify()/count()\n41\t    must transparently concatenate snapshot-front + sealed segments + active segment in seq order, no\n42\t    gap/dup at a seam, so all 34 consumers keep working unchanged.\n43\t13. **durable tip determines next_seq (no seq reuse)** — `_read_last_entry`/next_seq must resolve the tip\n44\t    from the ACTIVE segment (true global max seq).\n45\t14. **cross-writer serialization must cover the compactor** — spine_lock (RLock) + flock. CRITICAL: flock\n46\t    binds to the INODE, not the path; os.replace swaps the inode, so flock-on-target CANNOT exclude a\n47\t    cross-process appender. Need a PATH-STABLE lockfile (`spine.lock`) both append & compaction flock\n48\t    before resolving the active segment.\n49\t15. **monotonic-state-by-full-scan must survive** — per-device nonce high-water (envelope.py), newest\n50\t    warden_checkpoint per pubkey (cli.py), account.create cap count (actor_scope/actor), device\n51\t    authorize/revoke ledger (mesh/registry), kill-switch latch (killswitch), promotion grants\n52\t    (promotion), unresolved queued approvals (approvals), archivist supersession + promotion-key ledger\n53\t    (revise), open threads/commitments/contradictions + citations (queries.py). The\n5\t`cert_digest` is over the record's CONTENT only (scope/kind/source/actor/payload/\n6\tparent/supersedes) — NOT the wallclock `ts` — so the chain is replay-stable. Appends\n7\tare O(1): read the last line's entry, `append_entry`, write.\n8\t\"\"\"\n9\tfrom __future__ import annotations\n10\t\n11\timport json\n12\timport logging\n13\timport os\n14\timport threading\n15\tfrom contextlib import contextmanager\n16\tfrom pathlib import Path\n17\tfrom typing import Any, Iterator\n18\t\n19\tfrom ..config import SCOPE, SPINE_PATH\n20\tfrom ..reuse import ChainEntry, append_entry, build_chain, digest_payload, verify_chain\n21\tfrom .atomicio import fsync_dir\n22\tfrom .manifest import (\n23\t    Manifest,\n24\t    SpineLayout,\n25\t    initial_manifest,\n26\t    read_manifest,\n27\t    segment_filename,\n28\t    write_manifest,\n29\t)\n30\tfrom .models import SpineRecord, now_iso\n31\t\n32\ttry:\n33\t    import fcntl  # POSIX advisory file lock — cross-PROCESS append serialization\n34\texcept ImportError:  # pragma: no cover — non-POSIX\n35\t    fcntl = None  # type: ignore[assignment]\n36\t\n37\t_log = logging.getLogger(__name__)\n38\t\n39\t\n40\tclass SpineError(Exception):\n41\t    \"\"\"A structural spine fault — a corrupt/degenerate manifest or a manifest-referenced segment that\n42\t    cannot be read. Raised so the caller fails CLOSED rather than silently reading a truncated/empty\n43\t    chain (invariant 15: a state-scanner must never fail open).\"\"\"\n44\t\n45\t\n46\t# The record fields SpineRecord.from_dict needs; a line missing any of these is corrupt (skipped by\n47\t# reads, so a mid-file gap still surfaces via verify()).\n48\t_REQUIRED_KEYS = (\"seq\", \"scope\", \"kind\", \"source\", \"actor\", \"cert_digest\", \"prev_hash\", \"entry_hash\")\n49\t\n50\t_LOCKS_GUARD = threading.Lock()\n51\t_LOCKS: dict[str, \"threading.RLock\"] = {}\n52\t\n53\t\n54\tdef spine_lock(path: Path | str) -> \"threading.RLock\":\n55\t    \"\"\"A process-wide RE-ENTRANT lock per resolved spine path. Serializes `append` (read-tip → write)\n56\t    across threads so concurrent writers can't fork the hash chain, and — being re-entrant — lets a\n57\t    caller make a check-then-append atomic (e.g. the nonce replay gate) while its inner `append` still\n58\t    acquires the same lock. Cross-PROCESS serialization is added by an flock inside `append`.\"\"\"\n59\t    key = str(Path(path).resolve())\n60\t    with _LOCKS_GUARD:\n61\t        lk = _LOCKS.get(key)\n62\t        if lk is None:\n63\t            lk = _LOCKS[key] = threading.RLock()\n64\t        return lk\n65\t\n66\t\n67\tdef _last_nonempty_line(path: Path) -> str | None:\n68\t    \"\"\"Read the last VALID record line without loading the file (seek-from-end). A torn/garbage tail\n69\t    line — a partial write from a crash — is SKIPPED so a read/restart never blows up on it: we return\n70\t    the last line that JSON-parses and carries the chain fields (FIX 2). None if no valid line exists.\n71\t    For a clean file this returns exactly the last non-empty line, byte-for-byte as before.\"\"\"\n72\t    try:\n73\t        size = path.stat().st_size\n74\t    except OSError:\n75\t        return None\n76\t    if size == 0:\n77\t        return None\n78\t    window = 8192\n79\t    while True:\n80\t        start = max(0, size - window)\n81\t        with path.open(\"rb\") as f:\n82\t            f.seek(start)\n83\t            buf = f.read(size - start)\n84\t        lines = [ln for ln in buf.split(b\"\\n\") if ln.strip()]\n85\t        if start > 0 and lines:\n86\t            lines = lines[1:]                       # the first line may be partial unless we are at BOF\n87\t        for raw in reversed(lines):\n88\t            try:\n89\t                d = json.loads(raw)\n90\t            except ValueError:\n91\t                d = None\n92\t            if isinstance(d, dict) and all(k in d for k in _REQUIRED_KEYS):\n93\t                return raw.decode(\"utf-8\")\n94\t            _log.warning(\"spine: skipping malformed tail line while seeking the tip (%s)\", path)\n95\t        if start == 0:\n96\t            return None                             # scanned the whole file, no valid line\n97\t        window *= 4                                 # a torn tail bigger than the window (rare) — widen\n98\t\n99\t\n100\tdef _last_valid_boundary(path: Path) -> \"tuple[int, ChainEntry | None]\":\n101\t    \"\"\"Return (byte offset just past the last VALID record line, its ChainEntry) — bytes BEYOND that\n102\t    offset are a torn tail from an interrupted write, which `append` truncates before writing so the new\n103\t    record cannot merge with them and be silently lost (BLOCK-1). (0, None) for an empty / all-torn file.\n104\t    For a clean file the offset equals the file size (nothing to truncate).\"\"\"\n105\t    try:\n106\t        size = path.stat().st_size\n107\t    except OSError:\n108\t        return 0, None\n109\t    if size == 0:\n110\t        return 0, None\n111\t    window = 8192\n112\t    while True:\n113\t        start = max(0, size - window)\n114\t        with path.open(\"rb\") as f:\n115\t            f.seek(start)\n116\t            buf = f.read(size - start)\n117\t        segs: list[tuple[int, bytes]] = []          # (byte offset just past this line's \\n, line bytes)\n118\t        i = 0\n119\t        while True:\n120\t            nl = buf.find(b\"\\n\", i)\n121\t            if nl == -1:\n122\t                break\n123\t            segs.append((start + nl + 1, buf[i:nl]))\n124\t            i = nl + 1\n125\t        for end, raw in reversed(segs):             # near-EOF first; front partials (start>0) never win\n126\t            if not raw.strip():\n127\t                continue\n128\t            try:\n129\t                d = json.loads(raw)\n130\t            except ValueError:\n131\t                d = None\n132\t            if isinstance(d, dict) and all(k in d for k in _REQUIRED_KEYS):\n133\t                return end, ChainEntry(seq=d[\"seq\"], prev_hash=d[\"prev_hash\"],\n134\t                                       cert_digest=d[\"cert_digest\"], entry_hash=d[\"entry_hash\"])\n135\t            _log.warning(\"spine: skipping malformed tail segment while seeking the tip (%s)\", path)\n136\t        if start == 0:\n137\t            return 0, None\n138\t        window *= 4\n139\t\n140\t\n141\tclass SpineStore:\n142\t    def __init__(self, path: Path | str = SPINE_PATH) -> None:\n143\t        # `self.path` is the STABLE identity + lock token (…/spine.jsonl). After a migration it is renamed\n144\t        # away and no longer a data file, but it remains the key for the in-process RLock and the anchor\n145\t        # the whole layout derives from — so the RLock that `envelope.consume` shares with `append` stays\n146\t        # the same object across a rotation (invariant 14). All DATA I/O targets `self._active` (the\n147\t        # manifest's active segment, or — with no manifest yet — the legacy single file in place, which is\n148\t        # byte-identical to the pre-rotation store).\n149\t        self.path = Path(path)\n150\t        self.path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n151\t        self._layout = SpineLayout.for_path(self.path)\n152\t        # NB: `segments/` is created only when actually migrating/rotating (not on every construction), so a\n153\t        # legacy/fresh store leaves the spine dir byte-identical to today (no stray empty dirs).\n154\t        self._manifest: Manifest | None = read_manifest(self._layout)\n155\t        self._reconcile_orphan_migration()          # complete a migrate() interrupted before the manifest write\n156\t        self._active: Path = self._resolve_active_path()\n157\t        # seq -> byte-offset index (FIX 1). Built lazily on the first TARGETED read (get / iter_records\n158\t        # with since_seq >= 0) and then kept current: maintained O(1) on our own appends, and extended\n159\t        # (never rewritten — an append-only file's offsets never move) when a stat shows another PROCESS\n160\t        # grew the file. Full scans (verify/count/entries/iter_records(-1)) never touch it, staying a\n161\t        # single byte-identical pass. `_index_lock` guards the dict against concurrent read+append.\n162\t        # Slice 0/1: the index is single-segment (over `self._active`); it is (segment, offset)-aware in\n163\t        # a later slice. It is invalidated whenever the active segment changes under us.\n164\t        self._index_lock = threading.Lock()\n165\t        self._index_built = False\n166\t        self._offsets: dict[int, int] = {}\n167\t        self._max_seq = -1\n168\t        self._scan_pos = 0                          # byte offset just past the last COMPLETE line indexed\n169\t        self._last: ChainEntry | None = self._read_last_entry()\n170\t\n171\t    # --- segment layout / manifest ------------------------------------------------\n172\t    def _resolve_active_path(self) -> Path:\n173\t        \"\"\"The current append/read target. With a manifest, the active segment's absolute path; without\n174\t        one (a fresh or not-yet-migrated legacy spine), the legacy single file in place — byte-identical\n175\t        to the pre-rotation store, so nothing destructive happens until an explicit `migrate()`. A manifest\n176\t        that exists but names NO active segment is a corrupt/degenerate state and RAISES rather than\n177\t        silently falling back to `spine.jsonl` (which would resurrect a data file OUTSIDE the segment set —\n178\t        a fork; the guard also removes the same landmine for Slice-3 sealing, which must always leave an\n179\t        active segment).\"\"\"\n180\t        if self._manifest is not None:\n181\t            act = self._manifest.active()\n182\t            if act is None:\n183\t                raise SpineError(\"manifest has no active segment (corrupt/degenerate) — refusing to \"\n184\t                                 \"resurrect spine.jsonl outside the segment set\")\n185\t            return self._layout.seg_path(act)\n186\t        return self.path\n187\t\n188\t    def _read_target(self) -> Path:\n189\t        \"\"\"The file to read for THIS operation. Normally the cached active segment; if it has VANISHED (a\n190\t        migrate()/rotation in another process renamed it away), resolve the current active from the\n191\t        manifest for this read only — WITHOUT mutating shared state (lock-free reads must not race\n192\t        append's active update). Prevents a false-EMPTY read during the migrate instant, which for the\n193\t        nonce-highwater scan would regress the replay floor. In Slice 0 the only mover is migrate(), which\n194\t        RENAMES (byte-preserving), so the current index offsets stay valid for the resolved file. Fully\n195\t        epoch-invalidated, segment-spanning reads land in Slice 1.\"\"\"\n196\t        a = self._active\n197\t        if a.exists():\n198\t            return a\n199\t        m = read_manifest(self._layout)\n200\t        act = m.active() if m is not None else None\n201\t        if act is not None:\n202\t            r = self._layout.seg_path(act)\n203\t            if r.exists():\n204\t                return r\n205\t        return a\n206\t\n207\t    @contextmanager\n208\t    def _crossproc_lock(self) -> \"Iterator[None]\":\n209\t        \"\"\"Cross-process exclusion on the inode-stable lockfile (invariant 14 / D3). flock binds to the\n210\t        open file DESCRIPTION, not the path, so an os.replace of the manifest/segments cannot move the\n211\t        lock out from under a concurrent writer — which the prior flock-on-the-data-fd could not survive.\n212\t        Best-effort exactly as before (fcntl absent on non-POSIX; a flock failure is swallowed). NOT\n213\t        re-entrant across fds — never nest a second `_crossproc_lock` inside one (a second LOCK_EX on the\n214\t        same file from a different fd self-deadlocks); the seal-swap that runs inside append reuses the\n215\t        already-held lock rather than re-acquiring.\"\"\"\n216\t        if fcntl is None:  # pragma: no cover — non-POSIX\n217\t            yield\n218\t            return\n219\t        self._layout.lockfile_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n220\t        fd = os.open(str(self._layout.lockfile_path), os.O_RDWR | os.O_CREAT, 0o600)\n221\t        try:\n222\t            try:\n223\t                fcntl.flock(fd, fcntl.LOCK_EX)\n224\t            except OSError:  # pragma: no cover\n225\t                pass\n226\t            yield\n227\t        finally:\n228\t            os.close(fd)\n229\t\n230\t    def _complete_orphan_migration_locked(self) -> bool:\n231\t        \"\"\"MUST hold `_crossproc_lock`, and be called only when the manifest is currently ABSENT. If we are\n232\t        in the migrate() crash window — seg-0 present, `spine.jsonl` gone (the rename is atomic + durable),\n233\t        no manifest — finish the interrupted migration by publishing the manifest. Returns True iff it\n234\t        completed one. Called from BOTH __init__'s reconciler AND append's `_refresh_active_under_lock`, so\n235\t        a LIVE appender completes the orphan under the lock instead of re-creating `spine.jsonl` and forking\n236\t        the log (the critical review finding).\"\"\"\n237\t        seg0 = self._layout.segments_dir / segment_filename(0)\n238\t        if seg0.exists() and not self.path.exists():\n239\t            write_manifest(self._layout, initial_manifest(SCOPE, active_file=self._layout.segment_rel(0)))\n240\t            _log.warning(\"spine: completed an interrupted migration — published the manifest for %s\", seg0)\n241\t            return True\n242\t        return False\n243\t\n244\t    def _refresh_active_under_lock(self) -> None:\n245\t        \"\"\"MUST hold `_crossproc_lock`. FIRST complete any interrupted migration (so a live appender never\n246\t        resurrects `spine.jsonl`), THEN re-resolve the active segment from the manifest so an appender\n247\t        constructed before a concurrent migrate()/rotation writes to the CURRENT target — never a stale\n248\t        path (fork) or a renamed-away file (resurrection). One manifest read in the steady state.\"\"\"\n249\t        m = read_manifest(self._layout)\n250\t        if m is None and self._complete_orphan_migration_locked():\n251\t            m = read_manifest(self._layout)\n252\t        new_active = self.path\n253\t        act = m.active() if m is not None else None\n254\t        if act is not None:\n255\t            new_active = self._layout.seg_path(act)\n256\t        if new_active != self._active or (m is not None) != (self._manifest is not None):\n257\t            self._manifest = m\n258\t            self._active = new_active\n259\t            self._invalidate_index()\n260\t            self._last = self._read_last_entry()\n261\t\n262\t    def _invalidate_index(self) -> None:\n263\t        \"\"\"Drops the single-segment index so the next read rebuilds it against the (possibly changed)\n264\t        active segment.\"\"\"\n265\t        with self._index_lock:\n266\t            self._index_built = False\n267\t            self._offsets = {}\n268\t            self._max_seq = -1\n269\t            self._scan_pos = 0\n270\t\n271\t    def _reconcile_orphan_migration(self) -> None:\n272\t        \"\"\"Close the migrate() crash window at CONSTRUCTION. migrate() does an ATOMIC\n273\t        `os.replace(spine.jsonl → segments/seg-00000000.jsonl)` and THEN publishes the manifest; a crash\n274\t        BETWEEN them leaves seg-0 present, `spine.jsonl` gone, and no manifest — at which point the legacy\n275\t        fallback (active = spine.jsonl) would read the spine as EMPTY and silently hide all history. If we\n276\t        observe exactly that state, finish the (already-durable) migration under the lock. (A live appender\n277\t        does the same via `_refresh_active_under_lock`; a construction-time pass just recovers faster.)\"\"\"\n278\t        if self._manifest is not None:\n279\t            return\n280\t        seg0 = self._layout.segments_dir / segment_filename(0)\n281\t        if not (seg0.exists() and not self.path.exists()):\n282\t            return\n283\t        with self._crossproc_lock():\n284\t            if read_manifest(self._layout) is None:          # re-check under the lock\n285\t                self._complete_orphan_migration_locked()\n286\t            self._manifest = read_manifest(self._layout)\n287\t\n288\t    def change_token(self) -> tuple:\n289\t        \"\"\"A cheap, rotation-aware epoch token — (manifest generation, active file, size, inode) — that\n290\t        changes on ANY append (size), a migration/rotation (generation + active path/inode), or a reset.\n291\t        THE unified replacement for the size-only 'has the spine changed?' heuristic at every site\n292\t        (invariant 9 / A4) — notably the kill-switch verdict caches in `governor/killswitch.py` and\n293\t        `gesture/session.py`. Because it reads the manifest FRESH and keys on the resolved active segment\n294\t        (not the legacy `store.path`, which a migration renames away), a same-size rotation — or a\n295\t        migration performed by another process — can never serve a stale (un-halting) kill-switch verdict.\"\"\"\n296\t        m = read_manifest(self._layout)\n297\t        gen = m.generation if m is not None else -1\n298\t        active = self.path\n299\t        act = m.active() if m is not None else None\n300\t        if act is not None:\n301\t            active = self._layout.seg_path(act)\n302\t        try:\n303\t            st = active.stat()\n304\t            return (gen, str(active), st.st_size, st.st_ino)\n305\t        except OSError:\n306\t            return (gen, str(active), -1, -1)\n307\t\n308\t    def migrate(self) -> bool:\n309\t        \"\"\"Move a legacy single-file spine into the segment layout: rename `spine.jsonl` →\n310\t        `<stem>.segments/seg-00000000.jsonl` (atomic, same filesystem — O(1), no byte copy) and publish a\n311\t        single-active-segment manifest. Idempotent (a no-op once a manifest exists). Returns True iff it\n312\t        migrated. SPLIT-BRAIN SAFETY: run this only after every spine writer is on code that flocks the\n313\t        path-stable lockfile (this Slice-0 code) and re-resolves the active segment under that lock — a\n314\t        stale appender then completes/observes the migration instead of resurrecting `spine.jsonl`. After\n315\t        migration `spine.jsonl` is absent and is NEVER re-created as a data file (all data I/O targets the\n316\t        active segment).\"\"\"\n317\t        with spine_lock(self.path):\n318\t            with self._crossproc_lock():\n319\t                if read_manifest(self._layout) is not None:\n320\t                    self._refresh_active_under_lock()\n321\t                    return False\n322\t                self._layout.segments_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n323\t                seg0_abs = self._layout.segments_dir / segment_filename(0)\n324\t                if self.path.exists():\n325\t                    os.replace(self.path, seg0_abs)         # atomic rename (same fs); O(1), no copy\n326\t                    fsync_dir(self._layout.spine_dir)       # persist the SOURCE-dir unlink of spine.jsonl\n327\t                else:\n328\t                    seg0_abs.touch()                        # fresh spine: create the empty active segment\n329\t                fsync_dir(self._layout.segments_dir)        # persist the TARGET-dir creation of seg-0\n330\t                write_manifest(self._layout, initial_manifest(SCOPE, active_file=self._layout.segment_rel(0)))\n331\t                self._manifest = read_manifest(self._layout)\n332\t                self._active = self._resolve_active_path()\n333\t                self._invalidate_index()\n334\t                self._last = self._read_last_entry()\n335\t                return True\n336\t\n337\t    # --- write --------------------------------------------------------------------\n338\t    def append(\n339\t        self, *, kind: str, source: str, actor: str, payload: dict[str, Any],\n340\t        parent_id: int | None = None, supersedes_id: int | None = None,\n341\t        ts: str | None = None,\n342\t    ) -> int:\n343\t        content = {\n344\t            \"scope\": SCOPE, \"kind\": kind, \"source\": source, \"actor\": actor,\n345\t            \"payload\": payload, \"parent_id\": parent_id, \"supersedes_id\": supersedes_id,\n346\t        }\n347\t        cert_digest = digest_payload(content)  # wallclock-free\n348\t        # Serialize the whole read-tip → write so concurrent writers (threaded bridge server, gesture\n349\t        # daemon) can't both fork off a stale tip and break the chain. The in-process RLock is keyed on\n350\t        # the STABLE `self.path` (unchanged), so the check-then-append gate `envelope.consume` builds on\n351\t        # top of it stays atomic across a rotation. Re-read the TRUE tip from disk under the lock.\n352\t        with spine_lock(self.path):\n353\t            with self._crossproc_lock():                 # cross-process guard on the path-stable lockfile\n354\t                self._refresh_active_under_lock()        # pick up a concurrent migrate()/rotation; never fork\n355\t                active = self._active\n356\t                # binary append+read: lets us TRUNCATE a torn tail before writing (BLOCK-1 fix). `a+b`\n357\t                # creates the file if absent and — in append mode — every write still lands at EOF.\n358\t                with active.open(\"a+b\") as f:\n359\t                    # BLOCK-1: an interrupted write can leave torn bytes PAST the last valid record. Appending\n360\t                    # after them would MERGE (torn + new) into one unparseable line → the new record is silently\n361\t                    # lost, verify() stays green, and a lost kill-switch panic never halts the mesh. Truncate the\n362\t                    # dead tail back to the last valid record FIRST (this only removes never-committed garbage\n363\t                    # from an interrupted write; committed records are untouched). Evaluated on the ACTIVE\n364\t                    # segment under the lock (invariant 7 — the append target is always the true chain tip).\n365\t                    clean_end, last = _last_valid_boundary(active)\n366\t                    pre_size = os.fstat(f.fileno()).st_size\n367\t                    if pre_size > clean_end:\n368\t                        f.truncate(clean_end)\n369\t                        _log.warning(\"spine: truncated a %d-byte torn tail (interrupted write) before append (%s)\",\n370\t                                     pre_size - clean_end, active)\n371\t                    entry = append_entry([last], cert_digest) if last else build_chain([cert_digest])[0]\n372\t                    record = {\n373\t                        \"seq\": entry.seq, **content, \"ts\": ts or now_iso(),\n374\t                        \"cert_digest\": cert_digest, \"prev_hash\": entry.prev_hash, \"entry_hash\": entry.entry_hash,\n375\t                    }\n376\t                    line = json.dumps(record, ensure_ascii=False) + \"\\n\"\n377\t                    offset = clean_end                      # after any truncate, EOF == clean_end (where the line lands)\n378\t                    f.write(line.encode(\"utf-8\"))\n379\t                    f.flush()\n380\t                    os.fsync(f.fileno())                    # FIX 3: an ack'd append is durable across a crash\n381\t                    # FIX 1: keep the index current with NO re-scan when it is already exactly up to date.\n382\t                    # If another PROCESS appended in between, `_scan_pos < offset` and we skip here — the next\n383\t                    # read's `_ensure_index` extends from `_scan_pos`, picking up the gap records AND this one.\n384\t                    with self._index_lock:\n385\t                        if self._index_built and self._scan_pos == offset:\n386\t                            self._offsets[entry.seq] = offset\n387\t                            if entry.seq > self._max_seq:\n388\t                                self._max_seq = entry.seq\n389\t                            self._scan_pos = offset + len(line.encode(\"utf-8\"))\n390\t                self._last = entry\n391\t        return entry.seq\n392\t\n393\t    # --- read ---------------------------------------------------------------------\n394\t    def iter_records(self, *, since_seq: int = -1) -> Iterator[SpineRecord]:\n395\t        \"\"\"Records with seq > `since_seq`, in order. FIX 1: for since_seq >= 0 the index seeks straight\n396\t        to the first wanted line (O(records-returned), not O(file)); a full read (since_seq < 0) starts\n397\t        at byte 0 exactly as before. FIX 2: a line that fails to parse or lacks required keys is SKIPPED\n398\t        (a torn tail no longer crashes the read); a torn MIDDLE line becomes a seq gap that verify() fails\n399\t        on, so mid-file tampering is never silently hidden.\"\"\"\n400\t        target = self._read_target()\n401\t        if not target.exists():\n402\t            return\n403\t        start_off = self._start_offset_for(since_seq)\n404\t        with target.open(\"rb\") as f:\n405\t            if start_off:\n406\t                f.seek(start_off)\n407\t            for raw in f:\n408\t                if not raw.strip():\n409\t                    continue\n410\t                try:\n411\t                    rec = SpineRecord.from_dict(json.loads(raw))\n412\t                except (ValueError, KeyError, TypeError):\n413\t                    _log.warning(\"spine: skipping malformed line during iter_records (%s)\", self._active)\n414\t                    continue\n415\t                if rec.seq > since_seq:\n416\t                    yield rec\n417\t\n418\t    def get(self, seq: int) -> SpineRecord | None:\n419\t        \"\"\"A single record by seq — O(1) via the index (FIX 1), byte-identical to a full scan.\"\"\"\n420\t        if seq < 0 or not self._read_target().exists():\n421\t            return None\n422\t        self._ensure_index()\n423\t        with self._index_lock:\n424\t            off = self._offsets.get(seq)\n425\t        if off is not None:\n426\t            with self._read_target().open(\"rb\") as f:\n427\t                f.seek(off)\n428\t                raw = f.readline()\n429\t            try:\n430\t                rec = SpineRecord.from_dict(json.loads(raw))\n431\t            except (ValueError, KeyError, TypeError):\n432\t                return None\n433\t            return rec if rec.seq == seq else None\n434\t        # not indexed (beyond the tip, or a corrupt line was skipped) — bounded fallback (still index-seeked)\n435\t        for r in self.iter_records(since_seq=seq - 1):\n436\t            if r.seq == seq:\n437\t                return r\n438\t            if r.seq > seq:\n439\t                break\n440\t        return None\n441\t\n442\t    def tail(self, n: int) -> list[SpineRecord]:\n443\t        \"\"\"The last `n` records, seek-from-end (O(n) bytes, NOT O(file)) — for a bounded RECENT-window\n444\t        read on a large spine. Fewer than `n` if the spine is shorter. NOTE: a bounded window collapses\n445\t        a RAPID (recent) replay flood, but does NOT bound AGGREGATE replay bloat — a rotated pool of\n446\t        distinct bodies larger than the window each ages out and re-records. Only pair `tail()`-based\n447\t        dedup with a record-time freshness gate (or an independent bound); do not rely on it alone to\n448\t        close a bloat sink.\"\"\"\n449\t        target = self._read_target()\n450\t        if n <= 0 or not target.exists():\n451\t            return []\n452\t        with target.open(\"rb\") as f:\n453\t            f.seek(0, os.SEEK_END)\n454\t            pos = f.tell()\n455\t            buf = b\"\"\n456\t            while pos > 0 and buf.count(b\"\\n\") <= n:\n457\t                step = min(65536, pos)\n458\t                pos -= step\n459\t                f.seek(pos)\n460\t                buf = f.read(step) + buf\n461\t        recs: list[SpineRecord] = []\n462\t        for ln in [x for x in buf.split(b\"\\n\") if x.strip()][-n:]:\n463\t            try:\n464\t                recs.append(SpineRecord.from_dict(json.loads(ln)))\n465\t            except (ValueError, KeyError, TypeError):\n466\t                continue\n467\t        return recs\n468\t\n469\t    @property\n470\t    def next_seq(self) -> int:\n471\t        return (self._last.seq + 1) if self._last else 0\n472\t\n473\t    def count(self) -> int:\n474\t        return sum(1 for _ in self.iter_records())\n475\t\n476\t    # --- integrity ----------------------------------------------------------------\n477\t    def entries(self) -> list[ChainEntry]:\n478\t        return [\n479\t            ChainEntry(seq=r.seq, prev_hash=r.prev_hash, cert_digest=r.cert_digest, entry_hash=r.entry_hash)\n480\t            for r in self.iter_records()\n481\t        ]\n482\t\n483\t    def verify(self) -> tuple[bool, str]:\n484\t        \"\"\"Two-layer UNKEYED integrity: (1) BINDING — each record's payload still hashes to its\n485\t        stored cert_digest (catches silent payload edits); (2) CHAIN — the entries link cleanly\n486\t        (catches delete/reorder/entry tamper). This proves internal CONSISTENCY, not authenticity:\n487\t        a naive payload edit fails (1), and a mid-chain digest edit cascades an entry_hash/prev_hash\n488\t        break caught by (2) — BUT a writer who recomputes cert_digest+entry_hash for the tip (no\n489\t        successor to cascade into) or forward-cascades a fork produces a self-consistent chain that\n490\t        passes here. Resistance to a recompute-capable writer is the owner-SIGNED head's job\n491\t        (`checkpoint.verify_checkpoint`, Ed25519 + monotonic last_seq). Use this for corruption/\n492\t        naive-tamper detection; use the signed head for tamper-EVIDENCE.\"\"\"\n493\t        entries: list[ChainEntry] = []\n494\t        for r in self.iter_records():\n495\t            content = {\n496\t                \"scope\": r.scope, \"kind\": r.kind, \"source\": r.source, \"actor\": r.actor,\n497\t                \"payload\": r.payload, \"parent_id\": r.parent_id, \"supersedes_id\": r.supersedes_id,\n498\t            }\n499\t            if digest_payload(content) != r.cert_digest:\n500\t                return False, f\"binding break at seq {r.seq}: payload does not match cert_digest (record tampered)\"\n501\t            entries.append(ChainEntry(seq=r.seq, prev_hash=r.prev_hash, cert_digest=r.cert_digest, entry_hash=r.entry_hash))\n502\t        return verify_chain(entries)\n503\t\n504\t    def _read_last_entry(self) -> ChainEntry | None:\n505\t        # `_last_nonempty_line` skips a torn/garbage tail and returns the last VALID line (FIX 2), so a\n506\t        # crash mid-write can no longer block a restart. The returned line is guaranteed parseable.\n507\t        target = self._read_target()\n508\t        line = _last_nonempty_line(target) if target.exists() else None\n509\t        if not line:\n510\t            return None\n511\t        d = json.loads(line)\n512\t        return ChainEntry(seq=d[\"seq\"], prev_hash=d[\"prev_hash\"], cert_digest=d[\"cert_digest\"], entry_hash=d[\"entry_hash\"])\n513\t\n514\t    # --- seq -> byte-offset index (FIX 1) -----------------------------------------\n515\t    def _start_offset_for(self, since_seq: int) -> int:\n516\t        \"\"\"Byte offset to seek so a forward read yields exactly seq > since_seq. A full read\n517\t        (since_seq < 0) starts at 0 WITHOUT building the index — full scans stay a single byte-identical\n518\t        pass. Otherwise the index gives an O(1) seek to the line of seq (since_seq + 1).\"\"\"\n519\t        if since_seq < 0:\n520\t            return 0\n521\t        self._ensure_index()\n522\t        with self._index_lock:\n523\t            off = self._offsets.get(since_seq + 1)\n524\t            if off is not None:\n525\t                return off\n526\t            if since_seq + 1 > self._max_seq:\n527\t                return self._scan_pos              # nothing beyond the tip → the read yields nothing\n528\t        return 0                                    # below the min, or a skipped/corrupt gap → safe full scan\n529\t\n530\t    def _ensure_index(self) -> None:\n531\t        \"\"\"Build the index lazily (one unavoidable scan) and keep it current. Detects a file that GREW\n532\t        (our own or another PROCESS's appends — append-only ⇒ offsets never move, so we only EXTEND) and\n533\t        a file that SHRANK / was rewritten in place smaller (rebuild). Thread-safe under `_index_lock`.\"\"\"\n534\t        target = self._read_target()\n535\t        try:\n536\t            size = target.stat().st_size if target.exists() else 0\n537\t        except OSError:\n538\t            return\n539\t        with self._index_lock:\n540\t            if not self._index_built:\n541\t                self._offsets = {}\n542\t                self._max_seq = -1\n543\t                self._scan_pos = 0\n544\t                self._index_built = True\n545\t                self._scan_from(0, size)\n546\t            elif size > self._scan_pos:\n547\t                self._scan_from(self._scan_pos, size)      # extend forward over the newly-appended bytes\n548\t            elif size < self._scan_pos:\n549\t                self._offsets = {}                          # truncated/rewritten smaller → rebuild\n550\t                self._max_seq = -1\n551\t                self._scan_pos = 0\n552\t                self._scan_from(0, size)\n553\t\n554\t    def _scan_from(self, start: int, size: int) -> None:\n555\t        \"\"\"Index every COMPLETE (newline-terminated) record line in bytes [start, size). MUST hold\n556\t        `_index_lock`. A malformed complete line is left OUT of the index (so a mid-file gap still\n557\t        surfaces via verify()); a trailing partial line (no newline yet) is left for the next extend.\"\"\"\n558\t        if size <= start:\n559\t            return\n560\t        with self._read_target().open(\"rb\") as f:\n561\t            f.seek(start)\n562\t            chunk = f.read(size - start)\n563\t        consumed = 0\n564\t        while True:\n565\t            nl = chunk.find(b\"\\n\", consumed)\n566\t            if nl == -1:\n567\t                break                                       # trailing partial line — not complete yet\n568\t            raw = chunk[consumed:nl]\n569\t            line_off = start + consumed\n570\t            consumed = nl + 1\n571\t            if not raw.strip():\n572\t                continue\n573\t            try:\n574\t                d = json.loads(raw)\n575\t                if not all(k in d for k in _REQUIRED_KEYS):\n576\t                    raise KeyError\n577\t                seq = d[\"seq\"]\n578\t            except (ValueError, KeyError, TypeError):\n579\t                continue                                    # corrupt complete line — do not index it\n580\t            self._offsets[seq] = line_off\n581\t            if seq > self._max_seq:\n582\t                self._max_seq = seq\n583\t        self._scan_pos = start + consumed\n584\t"}]},"uuid":"f7a88df9-d975-410c-a47f-deb739d04484","timestamp":"2026-07-19T16:22:37.589Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"efe709a2-8597-46e7-a7df-80e37b17268a","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}

`.gz` suffix when gzip-sealed (Slice 4).\"\"\"\n242\t+    base = f\"{SEGMENT_STEM}{seg_id:08d}.jsonl\"\n243\t+    return base + \".gz\" if codec == \"gzip\" else base\n244\t+\n245\t+\n246\t+class Segment(BaseModel):\n247\t+    \"\"\"One segment in the manifest. `first_prev_hash`/`boundary_hash` are the chain-linkage the reader\n248\t+    CROSS-CHECKS against the segment's real first/last records — they are convenience, not authority.\"\"\"\n249\t+    model_config = ConfigDict(extra=\"ignore\")   # forward-compatible: tolerate fields a newer writer added\n250\t+    id: int = Field(ge=0)\n251\t+    file: str                                    # path RELATIVE to the spine dir, e.g. \"segments/seg-00000000.jsonl\"\n252\t+    codec: str = \"none\"                          # \"none\" | \"gzip\"\n253\t+    sealed: bool = False\n254\t+    first_seq: int = Field(ge=0)\n255\t+    last_seq: int | None = None                  # null while active\n256\t+    count: int | None = None                     # null while active; CONVENIENCE ONLY — never a tamper input\n257\t+    first_prev_hash: str = _GENESIS_PREV         # == the prior segment's boundary_hash (seg 0 == genesis)\n258\t+    boundary_hash: str | None = None             # == entry_hash(last_seq); null while active\n259\t+    bytes: int = 0\n260\t+    sha256: str | None = None                    # at-rest file integrity; NOT a chain digest\n261\t+\n262\t+    @field_validator(\"file\")\n263\t+    @classmethod\n264\t+    def _file_is_contained_relative(cls, v: str) -> str:\n265\t+        \"\"\"A segment `file` must be a plain relative path under the spine dir — never absolute and never\n266\t+        `..`-escaping. `get()`/`iter_records()`/`tail()` open segment files WITHOUT running verify(), so a\n267\t+        doctored manifest pointing `file` at `/etc/passwd` or `../../secret` would otherwise surface that\n268\t+        file's bytes AS spine records. Reject it at the model boundary so the read path stays fail-closed\n269\t+        even though the manifest itself is unsigned.\"\"\"\n270\t+        pp = PurePosixPath(v)\n271\t+        if not v or pp.is_absolute() or PurePosixPath(v.replace(\"\\\\\", \"/\")).is_absolute() or \"..\" in pp.parts:\n272\t+            raise ValueError(f\"segment file must be a contained relative path, got {v!r}\")\n273\t+        return v\n274\t+\n275\t+\n276\t+class Manifest(BaseModel):\n277\t+    model_config = ConfigDict(extra=\"ignore\")\n278\t+    schema_version: int = _SCHEMA_VERSION\n279\t+    generation: int = Field(default=0, ge=0)     # monotonic epoch; ++ on EVERY swap — the unified change token\n280\t+    scope: str = \"\"\n281\t+    segments: list[Segment] = Field(default_factory=list)\n282\t+    manifest_sig: str | None = None              # reserved for the deferred signed-manifest tier\n283\t+\n284\t+    def active(self) -> Segment | None:\n285\t+        \"\"\"The single unsealed (append) segment, if any. There is at most one.\"\"\"\n286\t+        act = [s for s in self.segments if not s.sealed]\n287\t+        return act[-1] if act else None\n288\t+\n289\t+    def sealed_in_order(self) -> list[Segment]:\n290\t+        return sorted((s for s in self.segments if s.sealed), key=lambda s: s.first_seq)\n291\t+\n292\t+    def ordered(self) -> list[Segment]:\n293\t+        \"\"\"All segments in seq order (sealed by first_seq, then the active tail).\"\"\"\n294\t+        out = self.sealed_in_order()\n295\t+        act = self.active()\n296\t+        if act is not None:\n297\t+            out.append(act)\n298\t+        return out\n299\t+\n300\t+\n301\t+@dataclass(frozen=True)\n302\t+class SpineLayout:\n303\t+    \"\"\"The on-disk layout derived from a spine data-file path (…/spine.jsonl). Every artifact is\n304\t+    NAMESPACED BY THE DATA FILE'S STEM (`spine.manifest.json`, `spine.lock`, `spine.segments/`) so two\n305\t+    stores whose data files merely SHARE a directory — e.g. the `tempfile.mktemp()` idiom that drops files\n306\t+    straight into /tmp — never collide on a manifest/lock/segments set. Without this, one migrated store's\n307\t+    `manifest.json` would be read by every other store in that directory and silently steer it onto the\n308\t+    wrong segment (a real cross-store contamination the review caught).\"\"\"\n309\t+    data_path: Path            # the legacy/identity path (…/spine.jsonl) — the stable lock/identity token\n310\t+    spine_dir: Path\n311\t+    segments_dir: Path\n312\t+    manifest_path: Path\n313\t+    lockfile_path: Path\n314\t+    trash_dir: Path\n315\t+\n316\t+    @classmethod\n317\t+    def for_path(cls, data_path: Path | str) -> \"SpineLayout\":\n318\t+        p = Path(data_path)\n319\t+        d = p.parent\n320\t+        ns = p.stem or p.name       # \"spine.jsonl\" -> \"spine\"; namespaces every derived artifact\n321\t+        return cls(\n322\t+            data_path=p, spine_dir=d, segments_dir=d / f\"{ns}.segments\",\n323\t+            manifest_path=d / f\"{ns}.manifest.json\", lockfile_path=d / f\"{ns}.lock\", trash_dir=d / f\"{ns}.trash\",\n324\t+        )\n325\t+\n326\t+    def seg_path(self, seg: Segment) -> Path:\n327\t+        \"\"\"Absolute path of a segment's file (its `file` is stored relative to the spine dir). The\n328\t+        `Segment.file` validator guarantees it is a plain relative path that cannot escape spine_dir.\"\"\"\n329\t+        return self.spine_dir / seg.file\n330\t+\n331\t+    def segment_rel(self, seg_id: int, codec: str = \"none\") -> str:\n332\t+        \"\"\"The manifest `file` value (relative to spine_dir) for a segment id — under the namespaced\n333\t+        segments dir, e.g. 'spine.segments/seg-00000000.jsonl'.\"\"\"\n334\t+        return f\"{self.segments_dir.name}/{segment_filename(seg_id, codec)}\"\n335\t+\n336\t+\n337\t+def read_manifest(layout: SpineLayout) -> Manifest | None:\n338\t+    \"\"\"Load the manifest, or None if absent. A present-but-unparseable manifest RAISES (fail closed) —\n339\t+    the atomic write path guarantees it is never torn, so a parse failure means genuine corruption, not a\n340\t+    partial write, and must never be silently treated as 'no manifest' (which would strand the segments).\"\"\"\n341\t+    mp = layout.manifest_path\n342\t+    if not mp.exists():\n343\t+        return None\n344\t+    return Manifest.model_validate_json(mp.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\"))\n345\t+\n346\t+\n347\t+def write_manifest(layout: SpineLayout, manifest: Manifest) -> None:\n348\t+    \"\"\"Atomically publish the manifest (temp→fsync→os.replace→dir-fsync). THE cutover commit instant.\"\"\"\n349\t+    layout.manifest_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n350\t+    atomic_write_text(layout.manifest_path, manifest.model_dump_json(), prefix=\".manifest-\")\n351\t+\n352\t+\n353\t+def initial_manifest(scope: str, *, active_file: str, first_seq: int = 0,\n354\t+                     first_prev_hash: str = _GENESIS_PREV) -> Manifest:\n355\t+    \"\"\"A single-active-segment manifest (generation 0) — the shape produced by a fresh store or a\n356\t+    migration of an existing single-file spine.\"\"\"\n357\t+    return Manifest(\n358\t+        generation=0, scope=scope,\n359\t+        segments=[Segment(id=0, file=active_file, codec=\"none\", sealed=False,\n360\t+                          first_seq=first_seq, first_prev_hash=first_prev_hash)],\n361\t+    )\n362\tdiff --git a/sigil/spine/store.py b/sigil/spine/store.py\n363\tindex 0ce9a79..2b8af2a 100644\n364\t--- a/sigil/spine/store.py\n365\t+++ b/sigil/spine/store.py\n366\t@@ -12,11 +12,21 @@ import json\n367\t import logging\n368\t import os\n369\t import threading\n370\t+from contextlib import contextmanager\n371\t from pathlib import Path\n372\t from typing import Any, Iterator\n373\t \n374\t from ..config import SCOPE, SPINE_PATH\n375\t from ..reuse import ChainEntry, append_entry, build_chain, digest_payload, verify_chain\n376\t+from .atomicio import fsync_dir\n377\t+from .manifest import (\n378\t+    Manifest,\n379\t+    SpineLayout,\n380\t+    initial_manifest,\n381\t+    read_manifest,\n382\t+    segment_filename,\n383\t+    write_manifest,\n384\t+)\n385\t from .models import SpineRecord, now_iso\n386\t \n387\t try:\n388\t@@ -26,6 +36,13 @@ except ImportError:  # pragma: no cover — non-POSIX\n389\t \n390\t _log = logging.getLogger(__name__)\n391\t \n392\t+\n393\t+class SpineError(Exception):\n394\t+    \"\"\"A structural spine fault — a corrupt/degenerate manifest or a manifest-referenced segment that\n395\t+    cannot be read. Raised so the caller fails CLOSED rather than silently reading a truncated/empty\n396\t+    chain (invariant 15: a state-scanner must never fail open).\"\"\"\n397\t+\n398\t+\n399\t # The record fields SpineRecord.from_dict needs; a line missing any of these is corrupt (skipped by\n400\t # reads, so a mid-file gap still surfaces via verify()).\n401\t _REQUIRED_KEYS = (\"seq\", \"scope\", \"kind\", \"source\", \"actor\", \"cert_digest\", \"prev_hash\", \"entry_hash\")\n402\t@@ -123,13 +140,27 @@ def _last_valid_boundary(path: Path) -> \"tuple[int, ChainEntry | None]\":\n403\t \n404\t class SpineStore:\n405\t     def __init__(self, path: Path | str = SPINE_PATH) -> None:\n406\t+        # `self.path` is the STABLE identity + lock token (…/spine.jsonl). After a migration it is renamed\n407\t+        # away and no longer a data file, but it remains the key for the in-process RLock and the anchor\n408\t+        # the whole layout derives from — so the RLock that `envelope.consume` shares with `append` stays\n409\t+        # the same object across a rotation (invariant 14). All DATA I/O targets `self._active` (the\n410\t+        # manifest's active segment, or — with no manifest yet — the legacy single file in place, which is\n411\t+        # byte-identical to the pre-rotation store).\n412\t         self.path = Path(path)\n413\t         self.path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n414\t+        self._layout = SpineLayout.for_path(self.path)\n415\t+        # NB: `segments/` is created only when actually migrating/rotating (not on every construction), so a\n416\t+        # legacy/fresh store leaves the spine dir byte-identical to today (no stray empty dirs).\n417\t+        self._manifest: Manifest | None = read_manifest(self._layout)\n418\t+        self._reconcile_orphan_migration()          # complete a migrate() interrupted before the manifest write\n419\t+        self._active: Path = self._resolve_active_path()\n420\t         # seq -> byte-offset index (FIX 1). It is invalidated whenever the active segment changes under us.\n427\t         self._index_lock = threading.Lock()\n428\t         self._index_built = False\n429\t         self._offsets: dict[int, int] = {}\n430\t@@ -137,6 +168,172 @@ class SpineStore:\n431\t         self._scan_pos = 0                          # byte offset just past the last COMPLETE line indexed\n432\t         self._last: ChainEntry | None = self._read_last_entry()\n433\t \n434\t+    # --- segment layout / manifest ------------------------------------------------\n435\t+    def _resolve_active_path(self) -> Path:\n436\t+        \"\"\"The current append/read target. With a manifest, the active segment's absolute path; without\n437\t+        one (a fresh or not-yet-migrated legacy spine), the legacy single file in place — byte-identical\n438\t+        to the pre-rotation store, so nothing destructive happens until an explicit `migrate()`. A manifest\n439\t+        that exists but names NO active segment is a corrupt/degenerate state and RAISES rather than\n440\t+        silently falling back to `spine.jsonl` (which would resurrect a data file OUTSIDE the segment set —\n441\t+        a fork; the guard also removes the same landmine for Slice-3 sealing, which must always leave an\n442\t+        active segment).\"\"\"\n443\t+        if self._manifest is not None:\n444\t+            act = self._manifest.active()\n445\t+            if act is None:\n446\t+                raise SpineError(\"manifest has no active segment (corrupt/degenerate) — refusing to \"\n447\t+                                 \"resurrect spine.jsonl outside the segment set\")\n448\t+            return self._layout.seg_path(act)\n449\t+        return self.path\n450\t+\n451\t+    def _read_target(self) -> Path:\n452\t+        \"\"\"The file to read for THIS operation. Normally the cached active segment; if it has VANISHED (a\n453\t+        migrate()/rotation in another process renamed it away), resolve the current active from the\n454\t+        manifest for this read only — WITHOUT mutating shared state (lock-free reads must not race\n455\t+        append's active update). Prevents a false-EMPTY read during the migrate instant, which for the\n456\t+        nonce-highwater scan would regress the replay floor. In Slice 0 the only mover is migrate(), which\n457\t+        RENAMES (byte-preserving), so the current index offsets stay valid for the resolved file. Fully\n458\t+        epoch-invalidated, segment-spanning reads land in Slice 1.\"\"\"\n459\t+        a = self._active\n460\t+        if a.exists():\n461\t+            return a\n462\t+        m = read_manifest(self._layout)\n463\t+        act = m.active() if m is not None else None\n464\t+        if act is not None:\n465\t+            r = self._layout.seg_path(act)\n466\t+            if r.exists():\n467\t+                return r\n468\t+        return a\n469\t+\n470\t+    @contextmanager\n471\t+    def _crossproc_lock(self) -> \"Iterator[None]\":\n472\t+        \"\"\"Cross-process exclusion on the inode-stable lockfile (invariant 14 / D3). flock binds to the\n473\t+        open file DESCRIPTION, not the path, so an os.replace of the manifest/segments cannot move the\n474\t+        lock out from under a concurrent writer — which the prior flock-on-the-data-fd could not survive.\n475\t+        Best-effort exactly as before (fcntl absent on non-POSIX; a flock failure is swallowed). NOT\n476\t+        re-entrant across fds — never nest a second `_crossproc_lock` inside one (a second LOCK_EX on the\n477\t+        same file from a different fd self-deadlocks); the seal-swap that runs inside append reuses the\n478\t+        already-held lock rather than re-acquiring.\"\"\"\n479\t+        if fcntl is None:  # pragma: no cover — non-POSIX\n480\t+            yield\n481\t+            return\n482\t+        self._layout.lockfile_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n483\t+        fd = os.open(str(self._layout.lockfile_path), os.O_RDWR | os.O_CREAT, 0o600)\n484\t+        try:\n485\t+            try:\n486\t+                fcntl.flock(fd, fcntl.LOCK_EX)\n487\t+            except OSError:  # pragma: no cover\n488\t+                pass\n489\t+            yield\n490\t+        finally:\n491\t+            os.close(fd)\n492\t+\n493\t+    def _complete_orphan_migration_locked(self) -> bool:\n494\t+        \"\"\"MUST hold `_crossproc_lock`, and be called only when the manifest is currently ABSENT. If we are\n495\t+        in the migrate() crash window — seg-0 present, `spine.jsonl` gone (the rename is atomic + durable),\n496\t+        no manifest — finish the interrupted migration by publishing the manifest. Returns True iff it\n497\t+        completed one. Called from BOTH __init__'s reconciler AND append's `_refresh_active_under_lock`, so\n498\t+        a LIVE appender completes the orphan under the lock instead of re-creating `spine.jsonl` and forking\n499\t+        the log (the critical review finding).\"\"\"\n500\t+        seg0 = self._layout.segments_dir / segment_filename(0)\n501\t+        if seg0.exists() and not self.path.exists():\n502\t+            write_manifest(self._layout, initial_manifest(SCOPE, active_file=self._layout.segment_rel(0)))\n503\t+            _log.warning(\"spine: completed an interrupted migration — published the manifest for %s\", seg0)\n504\t+            return True\n505\t+        return False\n506\t+\n507\t+    def _refresh_active_under_lock(self) -> None:\n508\t+        \"\"\"MUST hold `_crossproc_lock`. FIRST complete any interrupted migration (so a live appender never\n509\t+        resurrects `spine.jsonl`), THEN re-resolve the active segment from the manifest so an appender\n510\t+        constructed before a concurrent migrate()/rotation writes to the CURRENT target — never a stale\n511\t+        path (fork) or a renamed-away file (resurrection). One manifest read in the steady state.\"\"\"\n512\t+        m = read_manifest(self._layout)\n513\t+        if m is None and self._complete_orphan_migration_locked():\n514\t+            m = read_manifest(self._layout)\n515\t+        new_active = self.path\n516\t+        act = m.active() if m is not None else None\n517\t+        if act is not None:\n518\t+            new_active = self._layout.seg_path(act)\n519\t+        if new_active != self._active or (m is not None) != (self._manifest is not None):\n520\t+            self._manifest = m\n521\t+            self._active = new_active\n522\t+            self._invalidate_index()\n523\t+            self._last = self._read_last_entry()\n524\t+\n525\t+    def _invalidate_index(self) -> None:\n526\t+        \"\"\"Drops the single-segment index so the next read rebuilds it against the (possibly changed)\n527\t+        active segment.\"\"\"\n528\t+        with self._index_lock:\n529\t+            self._index_built = False\n530\t+            self._offsets = {}\n531\t+            self._max_seq = -1\n532\t+            self._scan_pos = 0\n533\t+\n534\t+    def _reconcile_orphan_migration(self) -> None:\n535\t+        \"\"\"Close the migrate() crash window at CONSTRUCTION. migrate() does an ATOMIC\n536\t+        `os.replace(spine.jsonl → segments/seg-00000000.jsonl)` and THEN publishes the manifest; a crash\n537\t+        BETWEEN them leaves seg-0 present, `spine.jsonl` gone, and no manifest — at which point the legacy\n538\t+        fallback (active = spine.jsonl) would read the spine as EMPTY and silently hide all history. If we\n539\t+        observe exactly that state, finish the (already-durable) migration under the lock. (A live appender\n540\t+        does the same via `_refresh_active_under_lock`; a construction-time pass just recovers faster.)\"\"\"\n541\t+        if self._manifest is not None:\n542\t+            return\n543\t+        seg0 = self._layout.segments_dir / segment_filename(0)\n544\t+        if not (seg0.exists() and not self.path.exists()):\n545\t+            return\n546\t+        with self._crossproc_lock():\n547\t+            if read_manifest(self._layout) is None:          # re-check under the lock\n548\t+                self._complete_orphan_migration_locked()\n549\t+            self._manifest = read_manifest(self._layout)\n550\t+\n551\t+    def change_token(self) -> tuple:\n552\t+        \"\"\"A cheap, rotation-aware epoch token — (manifest generation, active file, size, inode) — that\n553\t+        changes on ANY append (size), a migration/rotation (generation + active path/inode), or a reset.\n554\t+        THE unified replacement for the size-only 'has the spine changed?' heuristic at every site\n555\t+        (invariant 9 / A4) — notably the kill-switch verdict caches in `governor/killswitch.py` and\n556\t+        `gesture/session.py`. Because it reads the manifest FRESH and keys on the resolved active segment\n557\t+        (not the legacy `store.path`, which a migration renames away), a same-size rotation — or a\n558\t+        migration performed by another process — can never serve a stale (un-halting) kill-switch verdict.\"\"\"\n559\t+        m = read_manifest(self._layout)\n560\t+        gen = m.generation if m is not None else -1\n561\t+        active = self.path\n562\t+        act = m.active() if m is not None else None\n563\t+        if act is not None:\n564\t+            active = self._layout.seg_path(act)\n565\t+        try:\n566\t+            st = active.stat()\n567\t+            return (gen, str(active), st.st_size, st.st_ino)\n568\t+        except OSError:\n569\t+            return (gen, str(active), -1, -1)\n570\t+\n571\t+    def migrate(self) -> bool:\n572\t+        \"\"\"Move a legacy single-file spine into the segment layout: rename `spine.jsonl` →\n573\t+        `<stem>.segments/seg-00000000.jsonl` (atomic, same filesystem — O(1), no byte copy) and publish a\n574\t+        single-active-segment manifest. Idempotent (a no-op once a manifest exists). Returns True iff it\n575\t+        migrated. SPLIT-BRAIN SAFETY: run this only after every spine writer is on code that flocks the\n576\t+        path-stable lockfile (this Slice-0 code) and re-resolves the active segment under that lock — a\n577\t+        stale appender then completes/observes the migration instead of resurrecting `spine.jsonl`. After\n578\t+        migration `spine.jsonl` is absent and is NEVER re-created as a data file (all data I/O targets the\n579\t+        active segment).\"\"\"\n580\t+        with spine_lock(self.path):\n581\t+            with self._crossproc_lock():\n582\t+                if read_manifest(self._layout) is not None:\n583\t+                    self._refresh_active_under_lock()\n584\t+                    return False\n585\t+                self._layout.segments_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n586\t+                seg0_abs = self._layout.segments_dir / segment_filename(0)\n587\t+                if self.path.exists():\n588\t+                    os.replace(self.path, seg0_abs)         # atomic rename (same fs); O(1), no copy\n589\t+                    fsync_dir(self._layout.spine_dir)       # persist the SOURCE-dir unlink of spine.jsonl\n590\t+                else:\n591\t+                    seg0_abs.touch()                        # fresh spine: create the empty active segment\n592\t+                fsync_dir(self._layout.segments_dir)        # persist the TARGET-dir creation of seg-0\n593\t+                write_manifest(self._layout, initial_manifest(SCOPE, active_file=self._layout.segment_rel(0)))\n594\t+                self._manifest = read_manifest(self._layout)\n595\t+                self._active = self._resolve_active_path()\n596\t+                self._invalidate_index()\n597\t+                self._last = self._read_last_entry()\n598\t+                return True\n599\t+\n600\t     # --- write --------------------------------------------------------------------\n601\t     def append(\n602\t         self, *, kind: str, source: str, actor: str, payload: dict[str, Any],\n603\t@@ -149,48 +346,48 @@ class SpineStore:\n604\t         }\n605\t         cert_digest = digest_payload(content)  # wallclock-free\n606\t         # Serialize the whole read-tip → write so concurrent writers (threaded bridge server, gesture\n607\t-        # daemon) can't both fork off a stale tip and break the chain.
